T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
i
Supplement 3
Documentation of National Adaptations of
International Background Questionnaire Items
Populations 1 and 2
Overview
This supplement contains documentation of national adaptations of the international versions
of the student, teacher, and school background questionnaire items. See Chapter 2 of the
accompanying User Guide for a description of the TIMSS background questionnaires. The
international versions of the questionnaires are provided in Supplements 1 and 2 of the User
Guide.
This documentation provides users with a guide to evaluate the availability of internationally-
comparable data for use in secondary analyses involving the TIMSS contextual variables. The
documentation of national adaptations is presented in seven separate sections corresponding
to each of the Population 1 or Population 2 questionnaire types:
Section 1:
Population 1 - Student Background Questionnaire
Section 2:
Population 1 - Teacher Background Questionnaire
Section 3:
Population 1 - School Background Questionnaire
Section 4:
Population 2 - Student Background Questionnaire
Section 5:
Population 2 - Mathematics Teacher Background Questionnaire
Section 6:
Population 2 - Science Teacher Background Questionnaire
Section 7:
Population 2 - School Background Questionnaire
Each section contains specific national documentation about individual background items in
order of questionnaire item location. Documentation is included for all variables for which
there are some countries: (i) included in the database but the version of the question
administered was somewhat different from the international version or (ii) not included in the
database. For each item, the following information is provided:
• Questionnaire Item Location Reference (as presented in Table 7.3 of the User Guide)
• Question (an abbreviated version of the main question with any relevant subitems and
general notes)
• Table of National Adaptation Documentation (listed by country and by subitem)
The version of the question shown in the documentation in this supplement is a shortened
version included to communicate the basic information about what was asked in each item
and to aid in the interpretation of the specific documentation comments. For the exact form
of each question, see Supplements 1 and 2.

S U P P L E M E N T  3 
i
T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
The Table of National Adaptation Documentation for each item lists any countries for which
internationally-comparable data for the indicated item or subitems are either unavailable or
for which data are included with notes on differences between the national and international
versions of the questions. It should be noted that this documentation does not include any
cases where simple recodes of the national data were made to produce internationally-
comparable data in the international variables (e.g. option reversals). In the Tables of
National Adaptation Documentation, countries are given one of two different data codes with
the following definitions:
Code D:  National data for country are included in the international variable.
This code is used for items where the international version of the question allows for
some nationally-defined options or where the modified national version for specific
countries are considered appropriate for comparison of univariate statistics. For these
items, some brief documentation is included to describe the national versions of the
questions, to assist the user in data interpretation.
Code X:  National data for country are NOT included in the international
variable.
This code is used to refer to all items that were (i) not administered, (ii) not applicable,
or (iii) deleted for a variety of reasons (e.g. not internationally comparable, removed
per NRC request, or removed due to other data problems). A general comment is
included for the majority of these cases: ‘Question Not Administered or Data Not
Internationally Comparable’. Other comments may be included for some variables
where some description is required to explain the absence of data.
The documentation provided is as complete as possible given the information provided by the
NRCs and the resources available for reviewing the individual national adaptations. The
review of national adaptations was instituted for the majority of the background questionnaire
items and, in particular, for any of the items that were used for reporting the contextual data
in the international reports. There are some items, however, that received less scrutiny than
those contained in the international reports, including a few for which documentation about
national adaptations is not available, as indicated in the following sections.

S U P P L E M E N T  3 
T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
iii
General Documentation Issues for Student Questionnaire Items
Specialized Versus Non-Specialized Versions of the Population 2 Variables
For Population 2, there were two versions of the student questionnaire administered, as
discussed in Chapter 2: (i) a non-specialized version - SQ2 and (ii) a specialized version -
SQ2(s). Countries administered the version of the questionnaire that was most appropriate
based on the way science was taught to their Population 2 students:  SQ2 was used in
countries with general/integrated science classes; SQ2(s) was used in countries where science is
taught as separate subjects (biology, earth science, chemistry, physics).
There are three basic types of Population 2 background variables indicated by the following
questionnaire item locations:
SQ2-** 
= General student background questions or questions pertaining to
mathematics contained in both the specialized and non-specialized
versions.
SQ2G-** = Questions pertaining to general/integrated science contained in only the
non-specialized version.
SQ2S-** = Questions pertaining to separate science subject areas contained in only
the specialized version.
Students in each country have valid data only for those variables associated with the version
of the questionnaire administered in their country, as indicated in Table 2.8 of the User
Guide. The background variables related to general/integrated science (BSBS****) contain
data only for students in countries that administered the non-specialized form of the
questionnaire, while the variables related to the separate science subject areas of biology,
chemistry, earth science, and physics (BSBB****, BSBC****, BSBE****, and
BSBP****) contain data only for students in countries that administered the SQ2(s) form of
the questionnaire. For the variables associated with the other version of the questionnaire, the
student data in each country will be coded as ‘not administered’.
The classification of countries by questionnaire type shown in Table 2.8 of the User Guide is
based on whether the series of questions related to activities in science classes is asked about
integrated science classes or separate science subject areas. In a few cases, countries used a
modified version of the student questionnaire omitting some of the science subject areas,
combining subject areas, or combining questions from both the specialized and non-
specialized versions. As a result, these countries were handled somewhat differently in the
international database and report tables or figures. All of the special cases below, indicating
exceptions to the general questionnaire type, as well as some other countries that asked
questions for only some of the science subject areas are presented in the Tables of National
Adaptation Documentation for the affected student background variables.

S U P P L E M E N T  3 
iv
T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
Austria:  Although the non-specialized version was used for the majority of questions about
science classes, Austria used the specialized version of the questions related to liking the
sciences (SQ2S-21b,c,d) and usually doing well in the sciences (SQ2S-17b,c,d). They were
reported in separate science subject areas (biological science, earth science, and physical
science) in the tables and figures related to these questions.
Belgium (French): Although the specialized version was used for the majority of questions
about science classes, Belgium (French) used the non-specialized version of the questions
related to liking science (SQ2G-21b) and usually doing well in science (SQ2G-17b). They
were reported in integrated science in the tables and figures related to these questions. Also,
both the non-specialized and specialized versions of the other questions related to attitudes
about the sciences and classroom activities were administered. Students were told to respond
to questions about either integrated science (SQ2G-28,29,31,32) or the questions about
biology (SQ2S-29-32) and physics (SQ2S-41-44), depending on the structure of their
science instruction. Data are reported in both the integrated science column and the separate
subject area columns (biology and physics) in the tables and figures related to these
questions.
France:  France used the specialized version asking questions about two combined science
classes (biology/geology and physics/chemistry). The combined class data are included in the
international variables related to biology (SQ2S-17b; 21b; 29-32) and physics (SQ2S-17d;
21d; 41-44). Their data are reported in the separate subject area columns (biology and
physics) in the tables and figures related to these questions.
Greece: Although the specialized version was used for the majority of questions about
science classes, Greece used  the non-specialized version of the questions related to liking
science and usually doing well in science. Based on information provided by the NRC about
the specific science subjects taken in the upper and lower grades, these data were recoded to
specialist variables: the upper-grade student data were included in the physical science
variables (SQ2S-17d; 21d) and the lower-grade student data were included in the biological
science variables (SQ2S-17b; 21b). The upper-grade students were reported in physical
science in the tables and figures related to these questions.
Latvia: Latvia used the specialized version of questions related to liking the sciences and
usually doing well in the sciences for three modified subjects:  biology, chemistry and
physics. Data for physics and chemistry were combined into physical science (SQ2S-21d;
17d). Data were reported in biology and physics in the tables and figures related to these
questions.
Netherlands: The Netherlands used the specialized version of the questions about five science
subjects: biology, earth science, physics, chemistry, and physics/chemistry. In the variables
about liking the sciences and usually doing well in the science, the data for physics, chemistry,
and physics/chemistry were all combined into the physical science questions (SQ2S-21d;
17d). In the variables pertaining science attitudes and classroom activities, data for students in
physics and physics/chemistry were combined into physics (SQ2S-41-44), and the small
amount of data in chemistry were deleted (SQ2S-33-36). Data were reported in biology, earth
science and physics in the tables and figures related to these questions.

S U P P L E M E N T  3 
T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
v
Portugal:  Portugal used the specialized version asking questions about combined science
classes (natural science and physical science). The combined class data are included in the
international variables related to biology (SQ2S-17b; 21b; 29-32) and physics (SQ2S-17d;
21d; 41-44). Their data are reported in the separate subject area columns (biology and
physics) in the tables and figures related to these questions.
Slovenia: Slovenia used the specialized version of questions related to liking the sciences and
usually doing well in the sciences for three modified subjects:  biology, chemistry and
physics. Data for physics and chemistry were combined into physical science (SQ2S-21d;
17d). Data were reported in biology and physics in the tables and figures related to these
questions.
Sweden:  In Sweden, there is a system split between the lower and upper grades (lower grade
in upper-primary and the upper grade in lower secondary). They used the non-specialized
version for their lower-grade students and the specialized version for their upper-grade
students due to the different structure of their science classes at these two educational levels.
Other General Issues in the Student Background Variables
Philippines and South Africa:  Student background data are not included in the Population 2
international reports, but data are retained in the international database. No systematic review
of national adaptations was made for the student background variables.
Bulgaria:  No Population 2 student background data are included in the international data
files.

Although these countries do have data for some or all of these items, no national adaptation
documentation is available. In order to work with these variables, secondary users must check
the individual variables in the teacher background files for the countries of interest.
Mathematics and Science Questions in the Population 1 Teacher Questionnaires
At Population 1, a single international version of the teacher questionnaire was developed
including all questions pertaining to general background information, mathematics-related
questions, and science-related questions. This was done because, in general, students in
Population 1 are taught both mathematics and science by a single classroom teacher. In this
situation, all teacher background variables are expected to contain data for all of the
Population 1 teachers. In some countries, however, a portion or even all of the students were
taught mathematics and science by different teachers. Table S3.2 indicates the countries
where at least some of their Population 1 students are taught mathematics and science by
separate teachers.
Table S3.2
Population 1 Countries With Students Taught by Single Classroom or Separate
Mathematics and Science Teachers

S U P P L E M E N T  3 
viii
T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
In countries with this type of educational system at Population 1, a high percent of missing or
not applicable data may be included for the mathematics-related and science-related variables
for teachers of the other subjects. To correct for this problem, as discussed in Chapter 7 of the
User Guide, the IDSUBJCT variable was used to identify the appropriate teachers to include in
background data tables in the international mathematics and science reports for Population 1.
It is recommended that IDSUBJCT be used as a filter in analyzing the Population 1 teacher
data in order to avoid high percentages of missing or not applicable responses for some
countries  (IDSUBJCT values: 1 = mathematics-only; 2 = science-only; 3 =
mathematics/science).
In addition to the general issue of missing data affecting all countries with separate
mathematics and science teachers, there are some countries with missing data for either
teacher due to the questionnaire administration procedures used in their country. These
situations are listed below and also are indicated in the Tables of National Adaptation
Documentation:
Czech Republic:  Science-only teachers were not given questionnaires and their data are
missing from the teacher background file. Where appropriate, some general classroom-related
data were copied from the mathematics teachers for matching classes.
Hong Kong:  Only one questionnaire was completed per class. The first part was completed
by the mathematics teacher, and the second part by the science teacher. Therefore, general
background data are not available for the science teachers. Where appropriate, some general
classroom-related data were copied from the mathematics teachers for matching classes.
Israel:  Many mathematics teachers did not take the questionnaire due to administration
problems. All data for these teachers are missing from the teacher background variables.
Singapore:  Three separate versions of the Population 1 teacher questionnaire were
administered:  one for mathematics teachers, one for science teachers, and one for
mathematics/science teachers. Some questions were omitted from the science teacher version.
Where appropriate, some general classroom-related data were copied from mathematics
teachers for matching classes.

T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
ix
Teacher Background Variables Without National Adaptation Documentation
For the following types of Population 1 and Population 2 teacher background items, no
documentation was compiled regarding national adaptations:
• Questions about Textbooks Used  (TQ1-17; TQM2B4; TQS2B4)
• Questions About Mathematics or Science Topics Taught (TQ1-25A-U; TQ1-37A-X;
TQM2B12A-V; TQS2B12A-X)
• Opportunity to Learn Questions (Optional Section C of Population 2 Teacher
Questionnaire - TQM2C**, TQS2C**)
• Pedagogy Questions (Optional Section D of Population 2 Teacher Questionnaire -
TQM2D**, TQS2D**)
Other General Issues in the Teacher Background Variables
Bulgaria, Philippines, and South Africa:  No Population 2 teacher background data are
included in the international data files.

T I M S S  D A T A B A S E  U S E R  G U I D E 
General Documentation Issues for School Questionnaire Items
School Variables Pertaining to Lower-Grade and Upper-Grade Students
In general, one lower-grade and one upper-grade classroom was sampled in each school per
the TIMSS sample design. However, in some countries the lower- and upper-grade classes are
selected from different schools due to the structure of the educational system. In particular, in
Population 2, New Zealand, Norway, Philippines, South Africa, Sweden,  and Switzerland have
system splits between their lower and upper grades for some or all students (lower grade in
upper primary and upper grade in lower secondary). In cases where only one of the two
relevant grades are included in a school, questions pertaining to the other grade are not
applicable for these schools. Although most of the data issues related to this situation should
have been taken care of during data entry, attention is drawn to the following specific school
background questionnaire items pertaining to lower- or upper-grade students that could be
affected by any remaining unusual  patterns in responses:  SCQ1-16E-P; SCQ2-17E-P;
SCQ1-17A-M; SCQ2-18A-R; SCQ1-18A-E; SCQ2-19A-E; SCQ1-19A,B; SCQ2-20A,B;
SCQ1-24A-F; SCQ2-25A-F; SCQ1-25A-H; SCQ2-26A-H; SCQ1-26A-F; SCQ2-27A-F;
SCQ1-27A-H; SCQ2-28A-H. To ensure that no inappropriate data are included in the
analyses of any of these school background variables for combined samples of lower- and
upper-grade students,  the IDGRADER identification variable for each school, described in
Chapter 7 of the User Guide, may be used as a filter to include only those schools containing
the appropriate grades for analysis (IDGRADER values: 1 = lower-grade only; 2 = upper-
grade only; 3 = both grades).
Other General Issues in the School Background Variables
Bulgaria, Philippines, and South Africa:  No Population 2 school background data are
included in the international data files.
Hungary:  Only a single version of the school background questionnaire was administered to
both Population 1 and Population 2 schools, since grades 1-8 are contained in the same
schools in Hungary. This led to some interpretation problems with the questions pertaining to
lower- and upper-grade students, since explicit lower- and upper-grade levels were not
specified. Some recodes were made to include as much of the data as possible; however,
several variables were deleted as not internationally comparable. These data modifications are
indicated in the Tables of National Adaptation Documentation.
Kuwait:  No translation was made of the school background questionnaire. The international
English version was administered to the schools.

TQ1-3
QUESTION:
What is the highest level of formal education you have completed?
International response options:
   1 = <teacher training w/o completing secondary>
   2 = <secondary only>
   3 = <secondary + 1 or 2 year teacher training>
   4 = <secondary and 3 or 4 year teacher training>
   5 = <BA or equivalent + no teacher training>
   6 = <BA or equivalent + teacher training>
   7 = <MA/Ph.D. with no teacher training>
   8 = <MA/Ph.D. + teacher training>
(Some countries omitted or modified options in accordance with their national systems.)

SQ2-9
QUESTION 
9/1: How far in school did your mother go?
9/2: How far in school did your father go?
9/3: How far do you expect to go in school?
International response options:
   1 = finished primary school;
   2 = finished some secondary school;
   3 = finished secondary school;
   4 = some vocational/technical education after secondary school;
   5 = some university;
   6 = finished university;
   7 = I don’t know.
(Some countries omitted or modified options to be consistent with their own national systems.)
Questionnaire   
Item ID:
Country:   
Code:  
Comment:   
SQ2-9
Austria
D
Modified: recoded to international options. Options 1, 4, 5 not included. Option 2 
= Compulsory (grades 1-9); Option 3 combines 4 secondary tracks 
(apprenticeship, medium vocational, higher academic, higher vocational); Option 
6 = University (master’s).
SQ2-9
Belgium(Fl)
D
Option 4 not included.  Option 5 = Higher Education not part of university.
SQ2-9
Belgium(Fr)
D
Option 4 not included.  Option 5 = Higher Education not part of university.
SQ2-9
Canada
D
Option 4 = Some University or College; Option 5 = Finished University or College
SQ2-9
Cyprus
D
Modified: recoded to international options. Option 5 Not Included; Option 4 = 
Finished College (not equivalent to university); Option 6 combines Finished 
University and Post-Graduate Studies.
SQ2-9
Czech Republic
D
Option 2 = Finished Vocational Training or Secondary without  Maturita; Option 3 
= Finished Vocational Training or Secondary with Maturita.
SQ2-9
Denmark
D
Modified: recoded to international options. Options 1,4 not included. Option 2 
combines two lower-secondary levels (Basic School, 9 or 10 grades); Option 3 
combines two upper-secondary options: Gymnasium (hf, htx, hhx) and vocational 
(EUD). Option 5 = medium-cycle higher education; Option 6 = long university 
program.
SQ2-9
England
X
Question not administered or data not internationally comparable.
SQ2-9
France
D
Modified; recoded to international options. Option 1 = No school or primary; 
Option 2 = Lower secondary (college) or certificate of vocational aptitude (CAP). 
Option 3 combines two upper-secondary options: diploma of vocational studies 
(BEP-11 grades) and baccalaureate (BAC general, technical or professional - 12 
or 13 grades);  Option 4 not included; Option 5 = 2-3 years study after BAC 
(BTS, DUT, License); Option 6 = 4 years study after BAC (master’s).
SQ2-9
Germany
D
Modified; recoded to international options. Options 1,5 not included; Option 2 
combines two lower or inter. secondary levels (gr. 9-10); Option 3 combines two 
upper secondary tracks (apprenticeship or gymnasium); Option 4 = Tertiary non-
university; Option 6 = University or Tech. University.
SQ2-9
Greece
D

Population 2
Mathematics Teacher Background Questionnaire
Section 5

TQM2A3
QUESTION:
What is the highest level of formal education you have completed?
International response options:
   1 = <teacher training w/o completing secondary>
   2 = <secondary only>
   3 = <secondary + 1 or 2 year teacher training>
   4 = <secondary and 3 or 4 year teacher training>
   5 = <BA or equivalent + no teacher training>
   6 = <BA or equivalent + teacher training>
   7 = <MA/Ph.D. with no teacher training>
   8 = <MA/Ph.D. + teacher training>
(Some countries omitted or modified options in accordance with their national systems.)
Questionnaire 
Item ID:
Country:   
Code:   Comment:   
TQM2A3
Australia
D

   A: mathematics;

   D: earth science;

   F: biology;
   G: chemistry;
   H: physics
   I:  other subjects.
(Countries to include only those science subjects appropriate in their schools.)
